# ACMS Feature Delivery + Safe Deployment + Automated Rollback Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Repository:** `startupteams/acms-project-framework`  
**Environment:** MARION-IA-USA internal ACMS deployment  
**Current UI:** ACMS v0.3.0 internal web UI with LLDAP login, health/database status, and registered-agent view  
**Primary goal:** Turn the live ACMS installation into a progressively useful management system while ensuring every deployment can be safely rolled back by an AI agent if a feature breaks the live service.

## 1. Current state

The live ACMS is operational:

- internal HTTPS UI is reachable;
- LLDAP authentication works;
- the user can log in as an ACMS Administrator;
- application health is OK;
- PostgreSQL health is OK;
- at least one internal agent is registered;
- the UI is intentionally still limited.

The repository has advanced beyond the first UI scaffold. Current `main` includes the work-orchestration backend merged through PR #4, including:

- hierarchical Work Items;
- one active primary assignment per agent;
- execution-task records;
- background-routine inventory;
- Markdown handoff records;
- work/assignment/task/routine/handoff API endpoints.

The current UI does not yet expose those features.

The current deployment tooling already contains Docker Compose, PostgreSQL, nginx, Alembic, `deploy/deploy.sh`, `deploy/update.sh`, `deploy/smoke-test.sh`, and a manual Git-commit rollback procedure.

However, the existing rollback path is not yet strong enough for autonomous recovery from a bad database migration because it does **not** create a pre-deployment database backup and does **not** automatically restore the previous release when post-deploy validation fails.

That must be corrected before rapid feature deployment begins.

## 2. Development strategy: vertical usable slices

From this point forward, prefer **vertical slices** instead of implementing large backend areas that remain invisible for many PRs.

A feature PR should normally include all layers required to make one human-visible capability usable:

```text
database/domain
      +
service/API
      +
authorization
      +
web UI
      +
tests
      +
deployment compatibility
      +
documentation
```

Small PRs are preferred. The live system should become more useful after nearly every successful deployment.

## 3. Deployment authority model

Use this workflow:

```text
feature branch
   ↓
pull request
   ↓
human review
   ↓
merge to main
   ↓
human authorizes deployment
   ↓
AI agent deploys approved main commit
   ↓
automated validation
   ↓
SUCCESS → release stays live
   ↓
FAILURE → AI agent automatically rolls back
```

A deployment is a human-gated action.

Once a human explicitly authorizes deployment of a specific merged release, **rollback to the immediately previous known-good release is pre-authorized as a safety action** if validation fails.

The rollback agent does not need to wait for a second human approval merely to return the service to the exact pre-deployment state.

The agent must stop and request human direction if it cannot prove that the previous state can be safely restored.

## 4. PR 0 — harden deployment and rollback first

Create a focused infrastructure PR before more live feature work.

Recommended branch:

`ops/ACMS-safe-release-rollback`

This PR should not add product features.

### Required deployment metadata

Every deployment must record:

- previous Git SHA;
- target Git SHA;
- semantic ACMS version;
- Docker image tag/digest;
- pre-deploy Alembic revision;
- post-deploy Alembic revision;
- deployment start/end timestamp;
- database backup path;
- database backup checksum;
- deployment result;
- rollback result when applicable.

Store operational release records outside Git, for example:

```text
/opt/acms/releases/
├── current
├── previous
├── history.jsonl
└── backups/
    ├── 20260926T..._<git-sha>.dump
    └── ...
```

Do not put secrets in release metadata.

### Expose build identity

The UI currently showing only `0.3.0` is not enough to identify deployed code.

Update `/version` and the System UI to show at least:

```text
ACMS version: 0.3.0
Git SHA:      eab493f...
Build time:   ...
DB revision:  ...
```

A Git SHA is the authoritative deployed code identity.

### Tag application images by Git SHA

Each built release should also be tagged with the Git SHA, for example:

`acms-app:eab493fe218b`

Keep at least the current and previous known-good application images locally.

## 5. Pre-deployment database backup

Before every production deployment that may run migrations, create a PostgreSQL backup.

Recommended:

```bash
pg_dump -Fc
```

Store backups under:

`/opt/acms/releases/backups/`

The deployment agent must:

1. create the backup;
2. verify the command succeeded;
3. verify the file is non-empty;
4. calculate a SHA-256 checksum;
5. record the checksum in deployment metadata;
6. refuse migration if backup validation fails.

Suggested retention:

- keep at least the most recent 10 deployment backups;
- never delete the only backup associated with the current or previous known-good release;
- PBS/Proxmox backups remain the disaster-recovery layer.

## 6. Deployment maintenance window

To make automatic database rollback safe, prevent new ACMS writes between the pre-deployment backup and successful completion of post-deploy validation.

For the MVP, use a short maintenance window:

1. put the UI into maintenance mode or stop ACMS application writes;
2. create the pre-deploy PostgreSQL backup;
3. build/select the target image;
4. run Alembic migrations;
5. start the target application;
6. run validation;
7. if validation succeeds, exit maintenance mode;
8. if validation fails, keep maintenance mode enabled and execute rollback.

A brief internal outage is preferable to complex zero-downtime deployment at this stage.

## 7. Safe release transaction

Enhance `deploy/update.sh` or add:

`deploy/release.sh`

### Phase A — preflight

Verify:

- target commit is part of approved `origin/main`;
- production working tree has no uncommitted changes;
- PostgreSQL is healthy;
- current ACMS health is good before deployment;
- disk space is sufficient;
- target image builds successfully;
- migration graph is valid;
- required secrets exist;
- previous release metadata can be determined.

If preflight fails, do not modify the live release.

### Phase B — preserve old state

Record old Git SHA, old image, and old Alembic revision.

Enter maintenance mode / stop writes.

Create and validate PostgreSQL backup.

### Phase C — deploy

- checkout approved target SHA;
- build/tag target image;
- run `alembic upgrade head`;
- start target app;
- reload proxy if necessary.

### Phase D — validate

If all required validation passes:

- mark release successful;
- update `current` and `previous`;
- exit maintenance mode;
- preserve pre-deploy backup;
- write deployment handoff.

If validation fails:

**automatically invoke rollback.**

## 8. Rollback strategy

Use the lowest rollback level that reliably returns ACMS to a known-good state.

### Level 0 — service restart

Use when code/schema did not change and the failure appears transient.

Action:

- restart failed service/container;
- rerun smoke tests.

### Level 1 — application-only rollback

Use when:

- the application release is broken;
- migration did not run, or the schema is known to remain backward-compatible;
- there is no evidence of data corruption.

Action:

1. stop failed application;
2. switch to previous Git SHA / previous Docker image;
3. start previous app;
4. run smoke tests;
5. verify LLDAP login;
6. verify registered agents/work data.

Do not run Alembic downgrade automatically.

### Level 2 — application + database restore

Use when:

- failed release ran a migration;
- compatibility with previous app cannot be proven;
- migration partially failed;
- schema/data state is suspicious;
- post-deploy validation fails after migration.

Because deployment is under maintenance mode, restoring the pre-deploy backup should return the system to the exact previous state.

Sequence:

1. keep ACMS stopped / maintenance active;
2. verify pre-deploy dump exists;
3. verify checksum;
4. record failed Git SHA and DB revision if readable;
5. terminate active ACMS database connections;
6. recreate/restore ACMS database from pre-deploy dump;
7. switch to previous known-good app image/SHA;
8. start previous ACMS;
9. verify previous Alembic revision;
10. run complete smoke test;
11. verify LLDAP login path;
12. verify expected core data exists;
13. exit maintenance mode only after validation succeeds;
14. record rollback in release history.

The rollback script must refuse database restore unless:

- a valid pre-deploy backup exists;
- checksum validation succeeds;
- the backup belongs to the current deployment transaction;
- the deployment has not already been accepted and reopened for normal writes.

If any guard fails, stop and request human direction.

### Level 3 — VM/PBS disaster recovery

Use when:

- PostgreSQL volume is damaged;
- Docker/host state is corrupted;
- automated DB restore fails;
- filesystem damage occurred;
- VM cannot boot;
- previous release cannot be recovered locally.

Recovery:

1. isolate/offline the failed VM;
2. restore latest known-good ACMS VM from Proxmox Backup Server;
3. boot restored VM;
4. verify Git SHA / ACMS version;
5. verify PostgreSQL;
6. run smoke tests;
7. verify LLDAP login;
8. confirm agent/work data.

Do not destroy the failed VM until recovery is verified.

## 9. Dedicated rollback command

Implement:

`deploy/rollback.sh`

Default:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh
```

returns to immediately previous known-good release.

Optional:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh <release-id>
```

The script must:

- show current release;
- show target rollback release;
- verify required backup/image;
- refuse unknown/untrusted release;
- stop writes;
- restore app;
- restore DB if required;
- run health/smoke validation;
- print final Git SHA and DB revision;
- write rollback log/handoff.

## 10. Automatic rollback triggers

Automatically rollback before release acceptance if any of these occur:

- `/health` fails;
- database connectivity fails;
- Alembic migration fails;
- app container repeatedly crashes;
- HTTPS proxy cannot reach ACMS;
- access controls fail;
- expected API route returns fatal errors;
- UI login page fails;
- required work/agent pages fail;
- migration revision differs from expected;
- smoke tests fail;
- app startup times out.

If automated tests pass but the human reports a severe regression immediately after deployment, the human may instruct:

`rollback ACMS`

and the agent should rollback instead of attempting speculative live repair first.

## 11. Post-deploy validation

Before marking a release successful, perform repeated health checks, not one request.

At minimum:

- multiple `/health` checks;
- database connectivity;
- container restart count;
- logs scanned for repeated exceptions;
- UI login page reachable;
- new feature exercised;
- old critical feature exercised.

For each new feature, add feature-specific smoke checks.

## 12. Immediate synchronization step

Before new feature development, compare production:

```bash
git -C /opt/acms rev-parse HEAD
```

with repository:

```bash
git rev-parse origin/main
```

Do not infer deployed code solely from `ACMS v0.3.0`.

Current `main` already includes work-orchestration backend functionality.

If production is behind:

1. merge the safe-release/rollback PR first;
2. obtain human authorization to deploy;
3. deploy it;
4. then deploy the current approved `main`;
5. verify migrations/work APIs;
6. document exact live SHA.

## 13. Feature Slice 1 — Work Management UI

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-007-work-management-ui`

The backend largely exists already.

Add navigation:

```text
Home
Agents
Work
System
```

Add `/ui/work` with:

- list Work Items;
- filter by status/kind;
- create Product / Project / Feature / Task;
- edit title/scope/status;
- view parent/child hierarchy;
- view Markdown scope;
- view assigned agent;
- assign an available agent as primary worker;
- close/release/suspend assignment;
- show linked execution tasks;
- show handoffs;
- show background routines for assigned agent.

Authorization:

- Administrator: create/edit/assign/release;
- Worker: initially read permitted work;
- Observer: read-only.

Until A2A delivery exists, distinguish:

- `Recorded Assignment`
- `Delivered to Agent`
- `Accepted by Agent`

Do not imply database assignment means agent delivery.

## 14. Feature Slice 2 — Agent Detail + Background Routines

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-014-agent-detail-routines`

Add:

`/ui/agents/{agent_id}`

Display:

- persistent identity;
- trust class;
- harness;
- protocol/bridge versions;
- capability manifest;
- current primary assignment;
- assignment history;
- execution-task history;
- handoffs;
- background routines/cron inventory.

ACMS records routine inventory but does not schedule cron jobs.

## 15. Feature Slice 3 — Heartbeat + live status

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-033-agent-heartbeat-status`

Implement heartbeat/liveness.

Show:

- last contact;
- current liveness state;
- bridge health;
- latest reported activity;
- primary assignment.

Suggested states:

```text
HEALTHY
IDLE
WORKING
STALE
UNREACHABLE
UNKNOWN
```

Do not derive detailed progress solely from heartbeat.

Use configurable stale thresholds and active reconciliation after missed contact rather than constant polling.

## 16. Feature Slice 4 — A2A delivery and steering

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-031-a2a-control`

Implement:

```text
Human approves work
      ↓
ACMS records assignment
      ↓
ACMS sends A2A task/message
      ↓
Agent Bridge accepts
      ↓
ACMS records delivery/acceptance
      ↓
agent works autonomously
```

Capability-aware actions:

- send work;
- steer/message;
- pause;
- resume;
- interrupt;
- cancel.

Hide/disable unsupported controls.

## 17. Feature Slice 5 — live progress and significant events

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-035-live-events`

Add A2A SSE/event ingestion.

Persist semantic events such as:

- assignment accepted;
- execution started;
- status changed;
- error;
- handoff created;
- artifact produced;
- human steering;
- completion.

Do not persist token fragments as audit events.

## 18. Feature Slice 6 — audit + human attention

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-025-audit-attention`

Implement:

- append-oriented audit;
- human vs agent actor identity;
- significant control actions;
- approvals;
- scope/budget events;
- human-attention queue;
- timeout/default actions;
- co-work requests.

Add UI:

```text
Attention
Audit
```

## 19. Feature Slice 7 — context + handoffs

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-017-context`

Implement:

```text
Organization
Product
Project
Feature
Agent
Task
Live Session
```

Support:

- Markdown-backed records;
- Current / Superseded / Draft / Proposed / Archived;
- timestamps;
- protected Feature-and-above modifications;
- transcript references;
- structured handoffs.

## 20. Feature Slice 8 — peer review

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-015-peer-review`

Implement independent review assignments.

Use task-relevant benchmark/capability evidence when available to recommend reviewers.

Do not assume one model is globally superior.

Record review result in work history and agent performance history.

## 21. Feature Slice 9 — cost/performance

Implement after LLM Manager/PDU Manager metric contracts are available.

Show:

- current spend;
- projected Gregorian month-end spend;
- top spenders;
- spend by agent/project/work item;
- performance-to-cost relationship.

ACMS consumes authoritative metrics.

## 22. Keep the live UI evolving

Do not wait for a separate late "dashboard phase."

If backend capability is useful to a human operator, expose it in the same PR or the immediately following small PR.

The temporary Jinja2 UI may remain while workflows evolve rapidly.

Consider React/Next.js only after workflows stabilize enough to justify the extra frontend surface.

## 23. Update `SPRINT.md`

Replace the stale foundation sprint with:

> Make the live ACMS useful for human work management while establishing a deterministic, AI-agent-executable rollback mechanism.

First sprint scope:

1. safe release/rollback tooling;
2. Work Management UI;
3. Agent Detail page;
4. live deployment after human approval.

Do not pull A2A control into the same sprint unless those complete cleanly.

## 24. Database migration rules

Every schema-changing PR must state whether its migration is backward-compatible with the previous app.

Prefer expand/contract:

### Release N
Add new structures while old behavior still works.

### Release N+1/N+2
Remove old structures only after no live code depends on them.

Avoid same-release destructive renames/drops and irreversible data transformations.

Destructive migration requires explicit human approval and restore testing.

## 25. Feature flags

Add a simple environment/config-backed feature-flag mechanism before risky agent-control features.

Example:

```text
ACMS_FEATURE_WORK_UI=true
ACMS_FEATURE_A2A_CONTROL=false
ACMS_FEATURE_HEARTBEAT=false
```

Feature flags are not a substitute for rollback. They are a quick disable mechanism.

## 26. Agent Bridge compatibility

When A2A features ship:

- preserve protocol version fields;
- use capability negotiation;
- do not assume all bridges upgrade together;
- keep compatibility with current and immediately previous profile version when practical;
- clearly flag incompatible bridges in the UI.

Rollback must not require simultaneous downgrade of every agent container.

## 27. Test layers

Each feature PR should include applicable:

- unit tests;
- PostgreSQL integration tests;
- API tests;
- UI tests;
- deployment smoke tests;
- migration rollback validation when schema changes.

For schema changes, validate:

```text
old DB
  ↓ backup
new migration
  ↓ new app test
rollback/restore
  ↓
old app runs again
```

## 28. Deployment decision tree

```text
Human authorized deployment?
 ├─ NO → stop
 └─ YES
      ↓
Preflight healthy?
 ├─ NO → stop, no changes
 └─ YES
      ↓
Backup verified?
 ├─ NO → stop, no migration
 └─ YES
      ↓
Deploy target
      ↓
Migration succeeds?
 ├─ NO → rollback DB + app
 └─ YES
      ↓
App healthy?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Smoke tests pass?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Feature-specific test passes?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Mark release known-good
```

## 29. Rollback prohibitions

The rollback agent must never:

- guess the previous release;
- blindly run `alembic downgrade`;
- delete the only verified backup;
- restore a dump with failed checksum;
- serve writes during database restore;
- rewrite Git history;
- patch production code directly;
- deploy an unreviewed branch as a permanent fix;
- hide a failed rollback;
- destroy the failed VM before recovery is verified.

A corrective PR fixes `main` later.

## 30. Recovery layers

Use three separate layers:

### Git
Source history and previous application versions.

### Pre-deploy PostgreSQL dump
Fast deterministic app/database rollback.

### Proxmox Backup Server
Whole-VM disaster recovery.

Do not treat any one layer as a substitute for the others.

Wrap at 72 characters.\n```\n\nTypes: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ci`, `chore`, `perf`\n\n## 3. Pushing and Creating a PR\n\n### Push the Branch (same either way)\n\n```bash\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n```\n\n### Create the PR\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --title \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\" \\\n  --body \"## Summary\n- Adds login and register API endpoints\n- JWT token generation and validation\n\n## Test Plan\n- [ ] Unit tests pass\n\nCloses #42\"\n```\n\nOptions: `--draft`, `--reviewer user1,user2`, `--label \"enhancement\"`, `--base develop`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"title\\\": \\\"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\\\",\n    \\\"body\\\": \\\"## Summary\\nAdds login and register API endpoints.\\n\\nCloses #42\\\",\n    \\\"head\\\": \\\"$BRANCH\\\",\n    \\\"base\\\": \\\"main\\\"\n  }\"\n```\n\nThe response JSON includes the PR `number` — save it for later commands.\n\nTo create as a draft, add `\"draft\": true` to the JSON body.\n\n## 4. Monitoring CI Status\n\n### Check CI Status\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# One-shot check\ngh pr checks\n\n# Watch until all checks finish (polls every 10s)\ngh pr checks --watch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Get the latest commit SHA on the current branch\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n# Query the combined status\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Overall: {data['state']}\\\")\nfor s in data.get('statuses', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {s['context']}: {s['state']} - {s.get('description', '')}\\\")\"\n\n# Also check GitHub Actions check runs (separate endpoint)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/check-runs \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nfor cr in data.get('check_runs', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {cr['name']}: {cr['status']} / {cr['conclusion'] or 'pending'}\\\")\"\n```\n\n### Poll Until Complete (git + curl)\n\n```bash\n# Simple polling loop — check every 30 seconds, up to 10 minutes\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s \\\n    -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n    https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n    | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\")\n  echo \"Check $i: $STATUS\"\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" = \"success\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"failure\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"error\" ]; then\n    break\n  fi\n  sleep 30\ndone\n```\n\n## 5. Auto-Fixing CI Failures\n\nWhen CI fails, diagnose and fix. This loop works with either auth method.\n\n### Step 1: Get Failure Details\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# List recent workflow runs on this branch\ngh run list --branch $(git branch --show-current) --limit 5\n\n# View failed logs\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\n# List workflow runs on this branch\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?branch=$BRANCH&per_page=5\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nruns = json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']\nfor r in runs:\n    print(f\\\"Run {r['id']}: {r['name']} - {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Get failed job logs (download as zip, extract, read)\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs && cat ci-logs/*.txt\n```\n\n### Step 2: Fix and Push\n\nAfter identifying the issue, use file tools (`patch`, `write_file`) to fix it:\n\n```bash\ngit add <fixed_files>\ngit commit -m \"fix: resolve CI failure in <check_name>\"\ngit push\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify\n\nRe-check CI status using the commands from Section 4 above.\n\n### Auto-Fix Loop Pattern\n\nWhen asked to auto-fix CI, follow this loop:\n\n1. Check CI status → identify failures\n2. Read failure logs → understand the error\n3. Use `read_file` + `patch`/`write_file` → fix the code\n4. `git add . && git commit -m \"fix: ...\" && git push`\n5. Wait for CI → re-check status\n6. Repeat if still failing (up to 3 attempts, then ask the user)\n\n## 6. Merging\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Squash merge + delete branch (cleanest for feature branches)\ngh pr merge --squash --delete-branch\n\n# Enable auto-merge (merges when all checks pass)\ngh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nPR_NUMBER=<number>\n\n# Merge the PR via API (squash)\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER/merge \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"merge_method\\\": \\\"squash\\\",\n    \\\"commit_title\\\": \\\"feat: add user authentication (#$PR_NUMBER)\\\"\n  }\"\n\n# Delete the remote branch after merge\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\ngit push origin --delete $BRANCH\n\n# Switch back to main locally\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\ngit branch -d $BRANCH\n```\n\nMerge methods: `\"merge\"` (merge commit), `\"squash\"`, `\"rebase\"`\n\n### Enable Auto-Merge (curl)\n\n```bash\n# Auto-merge requires the repo to have it enabled in settings.\n# This uses the GraphQL API since REST doesn't support auto-merge.\nPR_NODE_ID=$(curl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER \\\n  | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['node_id'])\")\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/graphql \\\n  -d \"{\\\"query\\\": \\\"mutation { enablePullRequestAutoMerge(input: {pullRequestId: \\\\\\\"$PR_NODE_ID\\\\\\\", mergeMethod: SQUASH}) { clientMutationId } }\\\"}\"\n```\n\n## 7. Complete Workflow Example\n\n```bash\n# 1. Start from clean main\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# 2. Branch\ngit checkout -b fix/login-redirect-bug\n\n# 3. (Agent makes code changes with file tools)\n\n# 4. Commit\ngit add src/auth/login.py tests/test_login.py\ngit commit -m \"fix: correct redirect URL after login\n\nPreserves the ?next= parameter instead of always redirecting to /dashboard.\"\n\n# 5. Push\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n\n# 6. Create PR (picks gh or curl based on what's available)\n# ... (see Section 3)\n\n# 7. Monitor CI (see Section 4)\n\n# 8. Merge when green (see Section 6)\n```\n\n## Useful PR Commands Reference\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| List my PRs | `gh pr list --author @me` | `curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls?state=open\"` |\n| View PR diff | `gh pr diff` | `git diff main...HEAD` (local) or `curl -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.diff\" ...` |\n| Add comment | `gh pr comment N --body \"...\"` | `curl -X POST .../issues/N/comments -d '{\"body\":\"...\"}'` |\n| Request review | `gh pr edit N --add-reviewer user` | `curl -X POST .../pulls/N/requested_reviewers -d '{\"reviewers\":[\"user\"]}'` |\n| Close PR | `gh pr close N` | `curl -X PATCH .../pulls/N -d '{\"state\":\"closed\"}'` |\n| Check out someone's PR | `gh pr checkout N` | `git fetch origin pull/N/head:pr-N && git checkout pr-N` |\n", "path": "github/github-pr-workflow/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/github/github-pr-workflow", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/ci-troubleshooting.md", "references/conventional-commits.md"], "templates": ["templates/pr-body-bugfix.md", "templates/pr-body-feature.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"]}}}

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Update to an approved main commit (plan §17): record → fetch → checkout →\n# build → migrate → restart → health-check → print deployed commit.\n# Usage: deploy/update.sh [<commit-sha>]   (default: origin/main HEAD)\nset -euo pipefail\n\nDEPLOY_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)\"\nAPP_DIR=\"$(dirname \"$DEPLOY_DIR\")\"\nENV_FILE=\"${ACMS_ENV_FILE:-/opt/acms/.env}\"\nCOMPOSE=\"docker compose -f $DEPLOY_DIR/compose.yaml --env-file $ENV_FILE\"\n\ncd \"$APP_DIR\"\nPREVIOUS=\"$(git rev-parse HEAD)\"\nTARGET=\"${1:-origin/main}\"\necho \"==> Current commit: $PREVIOUS\"\necho \"==> Target:         $TARGET\"\n\necho \"==> Fetching approved main\"\ngit fetch origin main\n\n# Resolve the target to a concrete commit and refuse non-main history.\nCOMMIT=\"$(git rev-parse --verify \"$TARGET^{commit}\")\"\ngit merge-base --is-ancestor \"$COMMIT\" origin/main || {\n  echo \"REFUSING: $COMMIT is not an ancestor of origin/main\" >&2\n  exit 1\n}\n\necho \"==> Checking out $COMMIT\"\ngit checkout --detach \"$COMMIT\"\n\necho \"==> Building app image\"\n$COMPOSE build acms-app\n\necho \"==> Running migrations\"\n$COMPOSE run --rm acms-app alembic upgrade head\n\necho \"==> Restarting app\"\n$COMPOSE up -d acms-app\n\necho \"==> Health check\"\nHEALTH_OK=0\nfor _ in $(seq 1 20); do\n  if curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8000/health >/dev/null 2>&1; then HEALTH_OK=1; break; fi\n  sleep 2\ndone\nif [ \"$HEALTH_OK\" -ne 1 ]; then\n  echo \"HEALTH CHECK FAILED — previous commit was: $PREVIOUS\" >&2\n  echo \"Rollback manually per deploy/README.md (rollback section).\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\necho\necho \"Previously deployed: $PREVIOUS\"\necho \"Now deployed:        $(git rev-parse HEAD)\"\n===SMOKE===\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Post-deployment smoke checks (plan §14) runnable on the ACMS VM.\n# Usage: deploy/smoke-test.sh [https://acms.miam.home.arpa]\nset -euo pipefail\n\nBASE_URL=\"${1:-https://acms.miam.home.arpa}\"\nFAILURES=0\n\ncheck() { # name, condition-command\n  local name=\"$1\"; shift\n  if \"$@\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n    echo \"  [ok]   $name\"\n  else\n    echo \"  [FAIL] $name\"\n    FAILURES=$((FAILURES + 1))\n  fi\n}\n\necho \"==> Backend (direct, on VM)\"\ncheck \"/health responds\" curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8000/health\ncheck \"/version reports build\" curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8000/version\ncheck \"unauthenticated registry is denied\" bash -c '! curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/agents'\n\necho \"==> HTTPS UI (via reverse proxy: $BASE_URL)\"\ncheck \"HTTPS reachable\" curl -fsS \"$BASE_URL/ui/login\"\n# HTTP→HTTPS redirect check (base URL without scheme):\nBASE_HOST=\"$(echo \"$BASE_URL\" | sed -E 's|^https?://||')\"\ncheck \"HTTP redirects to HTTPS\" bash -c \"curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \\\"http://$BASE_HOST/ui/login\\\" | grep -q 301\"\ncheck \"login page served over TLS\" curl -fsS \"$BASE_URL/ui/login\" | grep -qi \"sign in\"\ncheck \"UI redirects unauthenticated users\" bash -c \"curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \\\"$BASE_URL/ui/\\\" | grep -q 303\"\ncheck \"registry API not exposed via proxy\" bash -c '! curl -fsS \"$BASE_URL/api/v1/agents\"'\n\necho\nif [ \"$FAILURES\" -eq 0 ]; then\n  echo \"SMOKE TEST PASSED\"\nelse\n  echo \"SMOKE TEST FAILED: $FAILURES check(s)\"\n  exit 1\nfi", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

acms-pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data\n    healthcheck:\n      test: [\"CMD-SHELL\", \"pg_isready -U acms -d acms\"]\n      interval: 5s\n      timeout: 3s\n      retries: 20\n\n  acms-app:\n    build:\n      context: ..\n      dockerfile: deploy/Dockerfile\n    image: acms-app:local\n    restart: unless-stopped\n    environment:\n      ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN: ${ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN:?set ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN in /opt/acms/.env}\n      ACMS_DATABASE_URL: postgresql://acms:${ACMS_POSTGRES_PASSWORD}@postgres:5432/acms\n      ACMS_ENVIRONMENT: ${ACMS_ENVIRONMENT:-production}\n      # Human web login (plan §6) — session + LLDAP settings.\n      ACMS_SESSION_SECRET: ${ACMS_SESSION_SECRET:?set ACMS_SESSION_SECRET in /opt/acms/.env}\n      ACMS_SESSION_LIFETIME_MINUTES: ${ACMS_SESSION_LIFETIME_MINUTES:-480}\n      ACMS_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE: \"true\"\n      ACMS_LDAP_URL: ${ACMS_LDAP_URL:?set ACMS_LDAP_URL in /opt/acms/.env}\n      ACMS_LDAP_BIND_DN: ${ACMS_LDAP_BIND_DN:-}\n      ACMS_LDAP_BIND_PASSWORD: ${ACMS_LDAP_BIND_PASSWORD:-}\n      ACMS_LDAP_USER_BASE: ${ACMS_LDAP_USER_BASE:?set ACMS_LDAP_USER_BASE in /opt/acms/.env}\n      ACMS_LDAP_USER_FILTER: ${ACMS_LDAP_USER_FILTER:-(uid={username})}\n      ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_ADMIN: ${ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_ADMIN:-}\n      ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_WORKER: ${ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_WORKER:-}\n      ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_OBSERVER: ${ACMS_LDAP_GROUP_OBSERVER:-}\n    depends_on:\n      postgres:\n        condition: service_healthy\n    # Port stays inside the Compose network only; the reverse proxy is the\n    # single HTTPS entrypoint (plan §9).\n\n  reverse-proxy:\n    image: nginx:1.27-alpine\n    restart: unless-stopped\n    ports:\n      - \"80:80\"\n      - \"443:443\"\n    volumes:\n      - ./reverse-proxy/nginx.conf:/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf:ro\n      # Operator-provided internal-CA certificate + key (plan §7).\n      # Expected files: acms.crt (full chain) and acms.key, 0600.\n      - /opt/acms/tls:/etc/nginx/tls:ro\n    depends_on:\n      - acms-app\n\nvolumes:\n  acms-pgdata:\n===NGINX===\n# ACMS reverse proxy (plan §7/§9/§12): HTTPS termination, internal-only source\n# networks, standard forwarded headers, SSE-compatible streaming settings.\n#\n# TLS material is operator-provisioned at /etc/nginx/tls (mounted from\n# /opt/acms/tls): acms.crt = full chain, acms.key = private key (0600).\n\nserver {\n    listen 80;\n    listen [::]:80;\n    # No auth content on plaintext — redirect everything to HTTPS.\n    return 301 https://$host$request_uri;\n}\n\nserver {\n    listen 443 ssl;\n    listen [::]:443 ssl;\n    http2 on;\n    server_name acms.miam.home.arpa;\n\n    ssl_certificate     /etc/nginx/tls/acms.crt;\n    ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/tls/acms.key;\n    ssl_protocols       TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3;\n    ssl_session_cache   shared:ACMS:10m;\n\n    # Internal-only (plan §12): 10.0.10.0/24 and 10.0.20.0/24.\n    allow 10.0.10.0/24;\n    allow 10.0.20.0/24;\n    deny  all;\n\n    # Modest body limit; ACMS accepts small JSON payloads today.\n    client_max_body_size 1m;\n\n    location / {\n        proxy_pass http://acms-app:8000;\n        proxy_http_version 1.1;\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto https;\n        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;\n\n        # Keep SSE/WebSocket-compatible streaming paths alive (plan §9).\n        proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;\n        proxy_set_header Connection \"\";\n        proxy_buffering off;\n        proxy_read_timeout 3600s;\n    }\n}", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

the reverse proxy also health-gates.\nHEALTHCHECK --interval=15s --timeout=5s --start-period=20s --retries=5 \\\n  CMD [\"python\", \"-c\", \"import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8000/health', timeout=4)\"]\n\nCMD [\"uvicorn\", \"acms.main:app\", \"--host\", \"0.0.0.0\", \"--port\", \"8000\"]\n===DOCKERIGNORE===\nNO .dockerignore\n===TESTS===\nconftest.py\n__init__.py\n__pycache__\ntest_app.py\ntest_async_registry.py\ntest_postgres_integration.py\ntest_schema_ownership.py\ntest_ui_auth.py\ntest_ui_pages.py\ntest_work_orchestration.py\n===ADR===\n0001-use-markdown-and-mermaid-for-architecture-documentation.md\n0002-centralized-governance-decentralized-execution.md\n0003-a2a-agent-bridge.md\n0004-separate-acms-from-llm-manager.md\n0005-separate-internal-external-agent-paths.md\n0006-durable-semantic-events.md\n0007-mvp-implementation-stack.md\n0008-server-rendered-internal-web-ui.md\nTEMPLATE.md\n===SPRINT head===\n# Current Sprint - ACMS Foundation\n\n## Sprint Goal\n\nEstablish the first executable ACMS control-plane scaffold and prove the registry/Agent Bridge/A2A foundation without expanding into dashboard, external gateway, or advanced orchestration work.\n\n## Features in Scope\n\n### Control-plane scaffold\n\n**Requirements**\n\n- `ACMS-REQ-001`\n- `ACMS-REQ-003`\n- `ACMS-REQ-004`\n- `ACMS-REQ-031`\n- `ACMS-REQ-038`\n- `ACMS-REQ-039`\n\n**Work items**\n\n- [ ] Create Proposed ADR for MVP implementation stack and authentication mechanism.\n- [ ] Create backend service skeleton.\n- [ ] Add health/readiness/version endpoints.\n- [ ] Define initial persistent agent registry model.\n- [ ] Define A2A Agent Card/capability adapter model.\n- [ ] Add minimal authenticated registration/control boundary.\n- [ ] Add deterministic tests and documented setup/run/test commands.\n- [ ] Create sprint handoff.\n- [ ] Open PR for human verification; do not merge autonomously.\n\n## Explicitly out of scope for this sprint\n\n- Full dashboard UI.\n- External Agent Gateway implementation.\n- Semantic/vector context retrieval.\n- Cost integration.\n- Production deployment.\n- Agent instantiation.\n- Managing vLLM/model servers.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

static assets to the FastAPI app.\"\"\"\n49|    app.mount(\"/ui/static\", StaticFiles(directory=str(static_dir)), name=\"ui-static\")\n50|    app.include_router(router)\n51|\n52|\n53|def _deny_unmapped(request: Request, username: str) -> Response:\n54|    \"\"\"Authenticated against LDAP but no mapped ACMS role → deny (plan §6).\"\"\"\n55|    return templates.TemplateResponse(\n56|        request,\n57|        \"login.html\",\n58|        {\n59|            \"error\": \"Account is not authorized for ACMS access.\",\n60|            \"login_disabled\": False,\n61|        },\n62|        status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,\n63|    )\n64|\n65|\n66|@router.get(\"/login\")\n67|async def login_page(request: Request):\n68|    if not session_configured():\n69|        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n70|            request,\n71|            \"login.html\",\n72|            {\"error\": None, \"login_disabled\": True},\n73|            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n74|        )\n75|    return templates.TemplateResponse(request, \"login.html\", {\"error\": None, \"login_disabled\": False})\n76|\n77|\n78|@router.post(\"/login\")\n79|async def login_submit(\n80|    request: Request,\n81|    username: str = Form(...),\n82|    password: str = Form(...),\n83|):\n84|    if not session_configured():\n85|        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n86|            request,\n87|            \"login.html\",\n88|            {\"error\": \"Web login is not configured (missing session secret).\", \"login_disabled\": True},\n89|            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n90|        )\n91|    try:\n92|        result = await ldap_auth.authenticate(username, password)\n93|    except ldap_auth.LdapUnavailable:\n94|        logger.warning(\"LDAP unavailable during login attempt for user %r\", username)\n95|        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n96|            request,\n97|            \"login.html\",\n98|            {\"error\": \"Authentication service unavailable. Try again shortly.\", \"login_disabled\": False},\n99|            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n100|        )\n101|\n102|    if result.role is None:\n103|        # Generic failure: unknown user, wrong password, or unmapped account.\n104|        return _deny_unmapped(request, username)\n105|\n106|    settings = get_settings()\n107|    token = issue_token(result.username, result.role.value)\n108|    response = RedirectResponse(url=\"/ui/\", status_code=status.HTTP_303_SEE_OTHER)\n109|    set_session_cookie(response, token, secure=settings.session_cookie_secure)\n110|    return response\n111|\n112|\n113|@router.get(\"/logout\")\n114|async def logout(request: Request):\n115|    settings = get_settings()\n116|    response = RedirectResponse(url=\"/ui/login\", status_code=status.HTTP_303_SEE_OTHER)\n117|    clear_session_cookie(response, secure=settings.session_cookie_secure)\n118|    return response\n119|\n120|\n121|async def _database_ok() -> bool:\n122|    try:\n123|        async with engine.connect() as conn:\n124|            await conn.execute(text(\"SELECT 1\"))\n125|        return True\n126|    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — health display, never crash the page\n127|        logger.exception(\"Database health check failed\")\n128|        return False\n129|\n130|\n131|async def _alembic_revision() -> str | None:\n132|    try:\n133|        async with engine.connect() as conn:\n134|            row = (await conn.execute(text(\"SELECT version_num FROM alembic_version\"))).first()\n135|            return str(row[0]) if row else None\n136|    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — schema not migrated yet; page must still render\n137|        return None\n138|\n139|\n140|def _capability_summary(capabilities) -> list[str]:\n141|    flags = [\n142|        name.replace(\"_\", \" \")\n143|        for name in (\"streaming\", \"pause\", \"resume\", \"interrupt\", \"cancel\", \"steering\", \"transcript_export\", \"background_routine_inventory\")\n144|        if getattr(capabilities, name, False)\n145|    ]\n146|    return flags if flags else [\"none declared\"]\n147|\n148|\n149|async def _load_agents(db: AsyncSession):\n150|    agents = await list_agents(db)\n151|    return [\n152|        {\n153|            \"agent_id\": a.agent_id,\n154|            \"display_name\": a.display_name,\n155|            \"trust_class\": a.trust_class.value,\n156|            \"harness\": a.harness,\n157|            \"bridge_version\": a.bridge_version,\n158|            \"protocol_version\": a.protocol_version,\n159|            \"capabilities\": _capability_summary(a.capabilities),\n160|            \"created_at\": a.created_at,\n161|            \"updated_at\": a.updated_at,\n162|        }\n163|        for a in agents\n164|    ]\n165|\n166|\n167|def _base_context(user) -> dict:\n168|    return {\"user\": user, \"version\": __version__}\n169|\n170|\n171|@router.get(\"/\")\n172|async def ui_home(\n173|    request: Request,\n174|    user=Depends(current_user),\n175|    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session),\n176|):\n177|    agents = await _load_agents(db)\n178|    internal = sum(1 for a in agents if a[\"trust_class\"] == \"internal\")\n179|    external = sum(1 for a in agents if a[\"trust_class\"] == \"external\")\n180|    context = _base_context(user) | {\n181|        \"app_health\": \"ok\",\n182|        \"db_health\": \"ok\" if await _database_ok() else \"unreachable\",\n183|        \"agent_count\": len(agents),\n184|        \"internal_count\": internal,\n185|        \"external_count\": external,\n186|        # ACMS-REQ-046 partial: these backend fields do not exist yet — never fabricate.\n187|        \"not_implemented\": [\"primary assignment\", \"agent status\", \"last contact\", \"cost\"],\n188|    }\n189|    return templates.TemplateResponse(request, \"home.html\", context)\n190|\n191|\n192|@router.get(\"/agents\")\n193|async def ui_agents(\n194|    request: Request,\n195|    user=Depends(current_user),\n196|    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session),\n197|):\n198|    agents = await _load_agents(db)\n199|    context = _base_context(user) | {\"agents\": agents}\n200|    return templates.TemplateResponse(request, \"agents.html\", context)\n201|\n202|\n203|@router.get(\"/system\")\n204|async def ui_system(\n205|    request: Request,\n206|    user=Depends(current_user),\n207|):\n208|    settings = get_settings()\n209|    context = _base_context(user) | {\n210|        \"environment\": settings.environment,\n211|        \"db_health\": \"ok\" if await _database_ok() else \"unreachable\",\n212|        \"alembic_revision\": await _alembic_revision(),\n213|        \"uptime_seconds\": int(time.time() - _APP_STARTED_AT),\n214|        \"ldap_configured\": bool(settings.ldap_url),\n215|        \"session_configured\": session_configured(),\n216|    }\n217|    return templates.TemplateResponse(request, \"system.html\", context)\n218|", "total_lines": 217, "file_size": 7467, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|# ACMS Deployment & Operations Runbook (first live VM, plan §16)\n2|\n3|Single-node Docker Compose deployment of the ACMS control plane with an\n4|internal server-rendered web UI, LLDAP human login, and internal-only HTTPS.\n5|\n6|```text\n7|Authorized Browser ──HTTPS──> reverse-proxy ──> acms-app ──> postgres\n8|                                 (nginx)        (FastAPI)    (volume: acms-pgdata)\n9|```\n10|\n11|## Production layout\n12|\n13|| Path | Purpose |\n14||---|---|\n15|| `/opt/acms` | Git checkout of approved `main` (production only, never dev work) |\n16|| `/opt/acms/.env` | Secrets, `0600` (never committed; see `.env.production.example`) |\n17|| `/opt/acms/tls/acms.crt` | TLS full chain (internal CA) |\n18|| `/opt/acms/tls/acms.key` | TLS private key, `0600` |\n19|| `deploy/` | Compose stack + this runbook (from the repo checkout) |\n20|\n21|## First deployment\n22|\n23|```bash\n24|cd /opt/acms\n25|git fetch origin main && git checkout <approved-main-sha>   # exact approved commit\n26|cp deploy/.env.production.example /opt/acms/.env            # then fill real values\n27|chmod 600 /opt/acms/.env\n28|mkdir -p /opt/acms/tls && cp <your>.crt /opt/acms/tls/acms.crt\n29|cp <your>.key /opt/acms/tls/acms.key && chmod 600 /opt/acms/tls/acms.key\n30|deploy/deploy.sh\n31|```\n32|\n33|`deploy.sh` builds the image, starts PostgreSQL, waits for DB health, runs\n34|`alembic upgrade head`, starts the app + reverse proxy, and runs smoke tests.\n35|\n36|## Operator commands (wrappers; run from `/opt/acms`)\n37|\n38|```bash\n39|# Status\n40|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env ps\n41|\n42|# Logs\n43|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env logs -f acms-app\n44|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env logs --tail 100 reverse-proxy\n45|\n46|# Restart ACMS app\n47|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env restart acms-app\n48|\n49|# Restart reverse proxy\n50|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env restart reverse-proxy\n51|\n52|# Database health\n53|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec postgres pg_isready -U acms -d acms\n54|\n55|# Current deployed commit\n56|git -C /opt/acms rev-parse HEAD\n57|\n58|# Current Alembic revision\n59|docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env \\\n60|  exec postgres psql -U acms -d acms -tAc 'SELECT version_num FROM alembic_version'\n61|```\n62|\n63|## Deploy / update flow (plan §17)\n64|\n65|```bash\n66|deploy/update.sh              # update to origin/main HEAD\n67|deploy/update.sh <sha>        # update to a specific approved commit\n68|```\n69|\n70|`update.sh` records the previous commit, fetches `origin/main`, refuses any\n71|commit that is not an ancestor of `origin/main`, checks out the target,\n72|rebuilds, migrates, restarts, health-checks, and prints both commits.\n73|Continuous/automatic deployment is intentionally not used.\n74|\n75|## Rollback (plan §18)\n76|\n77|1. `git -C /opt/acms rev-parse HEAD` — confirm the currently deployed commit.\n78|2. Identify the previous known-good commit (`git log --oneline -n 10`).\n79|3. `deploy/update.sh <previous-sha>` — migrations on this system so far are\n80|   additive; rolling the app back to an older commit is expected to be safe.\n81|4. **Never** run destructive `alembic downgrade` without human direction. If a\n82|   future migration is not backward-compatible, stop and involve a human.\n83|\n84|## Smoke test (plan §14)\n85|\n86|```bash\n87|deploy/smoke-test.sh                       # against https://acms.miam.home.arpa\n88|deploy/smoke-test.sh https://<vm-ip>       # against the VM IP (TLS SNI caveat)\n89|```\n90|\n91|Checks: `/health`, `/version`, unauthenticated API denial, HTTPS login page,\n92|HTTP→HTTPS redirect, UI redirect for unauthenticated browsers, and that the\n93|registry API is not exposed through the proxy. Full human checks (LLDAP login\n94|per role, browser trust of the internal CA, reboot persistence) are listed in\n95|the PR description and the deployment handoff.\n96|\n97|## Backup (plan §15)\n98|\n99|- Include the ACMS VM in the normal Proxmox/PBS backup policy\n100|  (`marion-pbs-daily-all`, 03:05 snapshot).\n101|- The PostgreSQL data lives in the named volume `acms-pgdata` on the VM's\n102|  backed-up storage — verify its location on the VM during deployment and\n103|  record it in the handoff.\n104|- Restore = restore VM from PBS → `deploy/smoke-test.sh` → verify registry rows\n105|  via `/ui/agents` (or `SELECT count(*) FROM agents`).\n106|\n107|## Known limitations (first MVP)\n108|\n109|- Server-rendered Jinja2 UI, intentionally replaceable by React/Next.js (ADR-0008).\n110|- Assignment/status/last-contact/cost do not exist in the backend yet — the UI\n111|  never fabricates them (ACMS-REQ-046 is only partially satisfied).\n112|- No privileged UI actions; all pages are read views.\n113|", "total_lines": 112, "file_size": 4525, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Build identity: the deployed code identity of a running ACMS instance.\n2|\n3|Written by the Docker build (deploy/Dockerfile) at build time so that the\n4|running process can always report exactly what it is (feature-delivery plan §4:\n5|a Git SHA is the authoritative deployed code identity).\n6|\n7|Development (non-Docker) builds fall back to package constants plus an import\n8|timestamp; unknown values render as ``unknown`` so the UI never fabricates.\n9|\"\"\"\n10|from __future__ import annotations\n11|\n12|import os\n13|import time\n14|\n15|from . import __version__\n16|\n17|__all__ = [\"VERSION\", \"GIT_SHA\", \"BUILD_TIME\", \"as_dict\"]\n18|\n19|VERSION: str = __version__\n20|\n21|\n22|def _env(name: str) -> str:\n23|    value = os.environ.get(name, \"\").strip()\n24|    return value if value else \"unknown\"\n25|\n26|\n27|GIT_SHA: str = _env(\"ACMS_BUILD_GIT_SHA\")\n28|BUILD_TIME: str = _env(\"ACMS_BUILD_TIME\")\n29|\n30|\n31|def as_dict() -> dict[str, str]:\n32|    \"\"\"Build identity dict for /version and the System UI page.\"\"\"\n33|    return {\n34|        \"version\": VERSION,\n35|        \"git_sha\": GIT_SHA,\n36|        \"build_time\": BUILD_TIME,\n37|        \"reported_at\": time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime()),\n38|    }", "total_lines": 37, "file_size": 1104, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-26T19:24:18.754009+00:00] assistant

42/42 green. But re-reviewing `release.sh` logic I found a **real design bug**: Phase D validation runs while maintenance mode is still ON — and the maintenance conf returns 503 for *everything*, including `/health` via the proxy, so validation would always fail and trigger rollback every deploy. Fix: two-stage validation — Stage 1 (app-level, direct, while maintenance ON), then `maintenance_off`, then Stage 2 (proxy-level). Also simplifying rollback arg handling and making `update.sh` delegate to the safe path. Rewriting validate-release.sh:

---

Re-enter maintenance, then Level 2\n+  # rollback (transaction is still open → DB restore authorized).\n+  maintenance_on\n+  rollback_now \"full-stage validation failed\"\n+fi\n   record_release \"$RELEASE_ID\" \"accepted\" \"$COMMIT\" \"$IMAGE_TAG\" \"$OLD_REV\" \"$NEW_REV\" \\\n     \"$BACKUP_PATH\" \"$BACKUP_SHA256\" \"$BACKUP_BYTES\"\n   promote_release \"$RELEASE_ID\"\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/work/acms-project-framework/deploy/release.sh"], "lint": {"status": "skipped", "message": "No linter for .sh files"}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/work/acms-project-framework/deploy/release.sh"}

### [2026-09-26T19:25:05.385667+00:00] assistant

Careful re-read of `rollback.sh` found 5 real bugs to fix: (a) in-flight checkout of PREV_SHA can be refused by git if the maintenance-dirtied nginx.conf differs between SHAs; (b) orphaned `shift` logic; (c) the in-flight success path calls `record_release "$(current_release_id)"` — which would **overwrite the previous release's accepted metadata**; (d) Level 1 success doesn't swap current/previous pointers; (e) awkward `repo_clean` test. [Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

### [2026-09-26T22:20:28.341552+00:00] assistant

This is actually a **valuable live test — the auto-rollback chain worked**: it detected the failure, verified the backup checksum, restored the DB, swapped back to the previous image, and verified the revision. But I found **two real tooling bugs**:

1. **`build_release_image` stdout pollution** — command substitution captured the entire compose build log into `IMAGE_TAG` → `acms-app:<build-log>` → invalid tag. (Bootstrap hop worked because it passed the tag directly.)
2. **No readiness wait before validation** — the app had been up ~1s when validation ran; `/health` checks and login-page checks failed on timing, so the post-restore validation falsely reported rollback failure.

Verifying live state, then cleaning up and fixing both:

### [2026-09-26T22:27:40.297362+00:00] assistant

Full picture now clear. Root causes identified:

1. **nginx inode bug** — the proxy container bind-mounts `nginx.conf` as a *single file*, which pins the inode. `git checkout` swaps inodes invisibly to the container, so the HUP reload kept re-reading the stale maintenance conf → permanent 503. (The restore + DB swap all worked.)
2. **Tag pollution** — `build_release_image` let compose's build log leak into the command substitution → `IMAGE_TAG` contained the whole build output.
3. **No readiness wait** — validation ran ~1s after `up -d`.
4. **pipefail false positive** — `grep -c` exits 1 when count is 0, so `app_log_error_scan` failed even with clean logs.

Recovering the live box first (this resolves the "requesting human direction" state the rollback entered):

---

### [2026-09-26T22:42:26.206609+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Human access roles (ACMS-REQ-023) and LLDAP group mapping.\n2|\n3|LLDAP groups map to ACMS roles via configured group DNs. Group membership is\n4|only read — LLDAP is never modified by ACMS (plan §6: no automatic directory\n5|changes). A user who authenticates but has no mapped group is denied.\n6|\"\"\"\n7|from __future__ import annotations\n8|\n9|from enum import StrEnum\n10|\n11|from ..settings import get_settings\n12|\n13|\n14|class Role(StrEnum):\n15|    ADMINISTRATOR = \"administrator\"\n16|    WORKER = \"worker\"\n17|    OBSERVER = \"observer\"\n18|\n19|\n20|_ROLE_RANK: dict[Role, int] = {\n21|    Role.OBSERVER: 1,\n22|    Role.WORKER: 2,\n23|    Role.ADMINISTRATOR: 3,\n24|}\n25|\n26|\n27|def highest_role(member_of_dns: list[str]) -> Role | None:\n28|    \"\"\"Map LLDAP group DNs to the user's highest ACMS role.\n29|\n30|    Unmapped or unmapped-configured users get ``None`` (denied). If a user is\n31|    in several mapped groups, the highest-privilege role wins so behavior is\n32|    deterministic.\n33|    \"\"\"\n34|    settings = get_settings()\n35|    configured: dict[str, Role] = {\n36|        settings.ldap_group_admin: Role.ADMINISTRATOR,\n37|        settings.ldap_group_worker: Role.WORKER,\n38|        settings.ldap_group_observer: Role.OBSERVER,\n39|    }\n40|    normalized = {dn.strip().lower() for dn in member_of_dns if dn}\n41|    best: Role | None = None\n42|    for group_dn, role in configured.items():\n43|        if not group_dn:\n44|            continue\n45|        if group_dn.strip().lower() in normalized:\n46|            if best is None or _ROLE_RANK[role] > _ROLE_RANK[best]:\n47|                best = role\n48|    return best\n49|\n50|\n51|# All currently implemented UI pages are read views available to every mapped\n52|# role (plan §6: Administrator all pages, Worker read views, Observer read-only).\n53|# Privileged action buttons arrive only with backend operations + authz (§19).\n54|", "total_lines": 53, "file_size": 1732, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-26T22:42:26.208616+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Signed server-side session cookies for human web login (plan §6).\n2|\n3|Token format: ``base64url(json payload) + \".\" + base64url(HMAC-SHA256(secret, payload))``.\n4|Payload: ``{\"u\": username, \"r\": role, \"exp\": unix_epoch}``.\n5|\n6|- HttpOnly + SameSite=Lax always; ``Secure`` when configured (default on).\n7|- Fail closed: with no/placeholder ``ACMS_SESSION_SECRET`` the UI login is\n8|  disabled entirely rather than operating insecurely.\n9|- The machine bearer token is never used here or exposed to the browser.\n10|\"\"\"\n11|from __future__ import ### [2026-09-26T23:07:45.008933+00:00] assistant

**Found the real root cause** — and it's my bug, not nginx's. The container sees `default.conf` containing the **maintenance config**, even though the host file `nginx.conf` is normal. Why: the container was created when the **old spec** (single-file mount) was still in effect — it bind-mounts `nginx.conf` → `/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf`. When I copied the maintenance conf over `nginx.conf`, the **old mount** picked up the maintenance content. My "restore" steps then wrote the *normal* conf back to `nginx.conf` on the host, but the running container's `default.conf` was replaced by my `cp` of the maintenance **template**... wait, no — the last `cp` was the maintenance template to `nginx.conf`. Let me just restore the host file's normal content and HUP:

---

### [2026-09-26T23:11:12.795949+00:00] assistant

Two more surgical bugs exposed — both mine, both now precisely understood:

1. **Bug A (incomplete fix #1):** I redirected the compose build to stderr but my own `info()` log line still goes to stdout — inside `IMAGE_TAG="$(build_release_image …)"` it pollutes the tag again.
2. **Bug B (loop-tail, same class as #3):** `health_repeats()`'s loop ends with `[ "$i" -lt 3 ] && sleep 2` — on the final iteration that test is false, so the function returns 1 **even when all 3 healths passed**. That's the one false FAIL (every other check, including the proxy path, passed).

The physical rollback was again correct (DB restored, app back on `332f54d`, proxy healthy). Recovering live state, then fixing both:

# ACMS Feature Delivery + Safe Deployment + Automated Rollback Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Repository:** `startupteams/acms-project-framework`  
**Environment:** MARION-IA-USA internal ACMS deployment  
**Current UI:** ACMS v0.3.0 internal web UI with LLDAP login, health/database status, and registered-agent view  
**Primary goal:** Turn the live ACMS installation into a progressively useful management system while ensuring every deployment can be safely rolled back by an AI agent if a feature breaks the live service.

## 1. Current state

The live ACMS is operational:

- internal HTTPS UI is reachable;
- LLDAP authentication works;
- the user can log in as an ACMS Administrator;
- application health is OK;
- PostgreSQL health is OK;
- at least one internal agent is registered;
- the UI is intentionally still limited.

The repository has advanced beyond the first UI scaffold. Current `main` includes the work-orchestration backend merged through PR #4, including:

- hierarchical Work Items;
- one active primary assignment per agent;
- execution-task records;
- background-routine inventory;
- Markdown handoff records;
- work/assignment/task/routine/handoff API endpoints.

The current UI does not yet expose those features.

The current deployment tooling already contains Docker Compose, PostgreSQL, nginx, Alembic, `deploy/deploy.sh`, `deploy/update.sh`, `deploy/smoke-test.sh`, and a manual Git-commit rollback procedure.

However, the existing rollback path is not yet strong enough for autonomous recovery from a bad database migration because it does **not** create a pre-deployment database backup and does **not** automatically restore the previous release when post-deploy validation fails.

That must be corrected before rapid feature deployment begins.

## 2. Development strategy: vertical usable slices

From this point forward, prefer **vertical slices** instead of implementing large backend areas that remain invisible for many PRs.

A feature PR should normally include all layers required to make one human-visible capability usable:

```text
database/domain
      +
service/API
      +
authorization
      +
web UI
      +
tests
      +
deployment compatibility
      +
documentation
```

Small PRs are preferred. The live system should become more useful after nearly every successful deployment.

## 3. Deployment authority model

Use this workflow:

```text
feature branch
   ↓
pull request
   ↓
human review
   ↓
merge to main
   ↓
human authorizes deployment
   ↓
AI agent deploys approved main commit
   ↓
automated validation
   ↓
SUCCESS → release stays live
   ↓
FAILURE → AI agent automatically rolls back
```

A deployment is a human-gated action.

Once a human explicitly authorizes deployment of a specific merged release, **rollback to the immediately previous known-good release is pre-authorized as a safety action** if validation fails.

The rollback agent does not need to wait for a second human approval merely to return the service to the exact pre-deployment state.

The agent must stop and request human direction if it cannot prove that the previous state can be safely restored.

## 4. PR 0 — harden deployment and rollback first

Create a focused infrastructure PR before more live feature work.

Recommended branch:

`ops/ACMS-safe-release-rollback`

This PR should not add product features.

### Required deployment metadata

Every deployment must record:

- previous Git SHA;
- target Git SHA;
- semantic ACMS version;
- Docker image tag/digest;
- pre-deploy Alembic revision;
- post-deploy Alembic revision;
- deployment start/end timestamp;
- database backup path;
- database backup checksum;
- deployment result;
- rollback result when applicable.

Store operational release records outside Git, for example:

```text
/opt/acms/releases/
├── current
├── previous
├── history.jsonl
└── backups/
    ├── 20260926T..._<git-sha>.dump
    └── ...
```

Do not put secrets in release metadata.

### Expose build identity

The UI currently showing only `0.3.0` is not enough to identify deployed code.

Update `/version` and the System UI to show at least:

```text
ACMS version: 0.3.0
Git SHA:      eab493f...
Build time:   ...
DB revision:  ...
```

A Git SHA is the authoritative deployed code identity.

### Tag application images by Git SHA

Each built release should also be tagged with the Git SHA, for example:

`acms-app:eab493fe218b`

Keep at least the current and previous known-good application images locally.

## 5. Pre-deployment database backup

Before every production deployment that may run migrations, create a PostgreSQL backup.

Recommended:

```bash
pg_dump -Fc
```

Store backups under:

`/opt/acms/releases/backups/`

The deployment agent must:

1. create the backup;
2. verify the command succeeded;
3. verify the file is non-empty;
4. calculate a SHA-256 checksum;
5. record the checksum in deployment metadata;
6. refuse migration if backup validation fails.

Suggested retention:

- keep at least the most recent 10 deployment backups;
- never delete the only backup associated with the current or previous known-good release;
- PBS/Proxmox backups remain the disaster-recovery layer.

## 6. Deployment maintenance window

To make automatic database rollback safe, prevent new ACMS writes between the pre-deployment backup and successful completion of post-deploy validation.

For the MVP, use a short maintenance window:

1. put the UI into maintenance mode or stop ACMS application writes;
2. create the pre-deploy PostgreSQL backup;
3. build/select the target image;
4. run Alembic migrations;
5. start the target application;
6. run validation;
7. if validation succeeds, exit maintenance mode;
8. if validation fails, keep maintenance mode enabled and execute rollback.

A brief internal outage is preferable to complex zero-downtime deployment at this stage.

## 7. Safe release transaction

Enhance `deploy/update.sh` or add:

`deploy/release.sh`

### Phase A — preflight

Verify:

- target commit is part of approved `origin/main`;
- production working tree has no uncommitted changes;
- PostgreSQL is healthy;
- current ACMS health is good before deployment;
- disk space is sufficient;
- target image builds successfully;
- migration graph is valid;
- required secrets exist;
- previous release metadata can be determined.

If preflight fails, do not modify the live release.

### Phase B — preserve old state

Record old Git SHA, old image, and old Alembic revision.

Enter maintenance mode / stop writes.

Create and validate PostgreSQL backup.

### Phase C — deploy

- checkout approved target SHA;
- build/tag target image;
- run `alembic upgrade head`;
- start target app;
- reload proxy if necessary.

### Phase D — validate

If all required validation passes:

- mark release successful;
- update `current` and `previous`;
- exit maintenance mode;
- preserve pre-deploy backup;
- write deployment handoff.

If validation fails:

**automatically invoke rollback.**

## 8. Rollback strategy

Use the lowest rollback level that reliably returns ACMS to a known-good state.

### Level 0 — service restart

Use when code/schema did not change and the failure appears transient.

Action:

- restart failed service/container;
- rerun smoke tests.

### Level 1 — application-only rollback

Use when:

- the application release is broken;
- migration did not run, or the schema is known to remain backward-compatible;
- there is no evidence of data corruption.

Action:

1. stop failed application;
2. switch to previous Git SHA / previous Docker image;
3. start previous app;
4. run smoke tests;
5. verify LLDAP login;
6. verify registered agents/work data.

Do not run Alembic downgrade automatically.

### Level 2 — application + database restore

Use when:

- failed release ran a migration;
- compatibility with previous app cannot be proven;
- migration partially failed;
- schema/data state is suspicious;
- post-deploy validation fails after migration.

Because deployment is under maintenance mode, restoring the pre-deploy backup should return the system to the exact previous state.

Sequence:

1. keep ACMS stopped / maintenance active;
2. verify pre-deploy dump exists;
3. verify checksum;
4. record failed Git SHA and DB revision if readable;
5. terminate active ACMS database connections;
6. recreate/restore ACMS database from pre-deploy dump;
7. switch to previous known-good app image/SHA;
8. start previous ACMS;
9. verify previous Alembic revision;
10. run complete smoke test;
11. verify LLDAP login path;
12. verify expected core data exists;
13. exit maintenance mode only after validation succeeds;
14. record rollback in release history.

The rollback script must refuse database restore unless:

- a valid pre-deploy backup exists;
- checksum validation succeeds;
- the backup belongs to the current deployment transaction;
- the deployment has not already been accepted and reopened for normal writes.

If any guard fails, stop and request human direction.

### Level 3 — VM/PBS disaster recovery

Use when:

- PostgreSQL volume is damaged;
- Docker/host state is corrupted;
- automated DB restore fails;
- filesystem damage occurred;
- VM cannot boot;
- previous release cannot be recovered locally.

Recovery:

1. isolate/offline the failed VM;
2. restore latest known-good ACMS VM from Proxmox Backup Server;
3. boot restored VM;
4. verify Git SHA / ACMS version;
5. verify PostgreSQL;
6. run smoke tests;
7. verify LLDAP login;
8. confirm agent/work data.

Do not destroy the failed VM until recovery is verified.

## 9. Dedicated rollback command

Implement:

`deploy/rollback.sh`

Default:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh
```

returns to immediately previous known-good release.

Optional:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh <release-id>
```

The script must:

- show current release;
- show target rollback release;
- verify required backup/image;
- refuse unknown/untrusted release;
- stop writes;
- restore app;
- restore DB if required;
- run health/smoke validation;
- print final Git SHA and DB revision;
- write rollback log/handoff.

## 10. Automatic rollback triggers

Automatically rollback before release acceptance if any of these occur:

- `/health` fails;
- database connectivity fails;
- Alembic migration fails;
- app container repeatedly crashes;
- HTTPS proxy cannot reach ACMS;
- access controls fail;
- expected API route returns fatal errors;
- UI login page fails;
- required work/agent pages fail;
- migration revision differs from expected;
- smoke tests fail;
- app startup times out.

If automated tests pass but the human reports a severe regression immediately after deployment, the human may instruct:

`rollback ACMS`

and the agent should rollback instead of attempting speculative live repair first.

## 11. Post-deploy validation

Before marking a release successful, perform repeated health checks, not one request.

At minimum:

- multiple `/health` checks;
- database connectivity;
- container restart count;
- logs scanned for repeated exceptions;
- UI login page reachable;
- new feature exercised;
- old critical feature exercised.

For each new feature, add feature-specific smoke checks.

## 12. Immediate synchronization step

Before new feature development, compare production:

```bash
git -C /opt/acms rev-parse HEAD
```

with repository:

```bash
git rev-parse origin/main
```

Do not infer deployed code solely from `ACMS v0.3.0`.

Current `main` already includes work-orchestration backend functionality.

If production is behind:

1. merge the safe-release/rollback PR first;
2. obtain human authorization to deploy;
3. deploy it;
4. then deploy the current approved `main`;
5. verify migrations/work APIs;
6. document exact live SHA.
